Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

1 Samuel 1:1

There was a man from Armathaim Sipha in the hill country of Ephraim. His name was Elkanah, son of Jerahmeel, son of Elihu, son of Thoke, in Nasib Ephraim.

  1. 1 Samuel 1:19 Same-chapter Elkanah's introduction and return to his named home

    Elkanah is introduced from Armathaim Sipha in Ephraim's hill country; the family's return later names Elkanah's house at Armathaim, connecting the journey with its introduced household.

    Read matching passage

    1 Samuel 1:19 They rose early in the morning, bowed down to the Lord, and went on their way. Elkanah entered his house at Armathaim and knew Hannah his wife, and the Lord remembered her,

    Pinned source record

1 Samuel 1:2

He had two wives. One was named Hannah, and the second was named Peninnah. Peninnah had children, but Hannah had no child.

  1. 1 Samuel 1:5-6 Same-chapter childlessness developed in Hannah's affliction

    The household introduction distinguishes Peninnah's children and Hannah's childlessness; the following account describes Hannah's portion, Elkanah's love and her distress because the Lord had closed her womb.

    Read matching passage

    1 Samuel 1:5 To Hannah he gave one portion, because she had no child. Yet Elkanah loved Hannah more than this other woman, and the Lord had closed her womb.

    1 Samuel 1:6 For the Lord had not given her a child, in her affliction and in the despondency of her affliction. She was despondent because of this: the Lord had shut her womb so as not to give her a child.

    Pinned source record

1 Samuel 1:3

The man went up year by year from his city, from Armathaim, to bow down and sacrifice to the Lord God Sabaoth at Shiloh. Eli and his two sons, Hophni and Phinehas, were there as priests of the Lord.

  1. Joshua 18:1 Shiloh's worship setting and its earlier tabernacle assembly

    Elkanah's annual worship is at Shiloh, where Eli's family serves as priests; Joshua records the congregation assembling and pitching the Tabernacle of Testimony there.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 18:1 The whole congregation of the sons of Israel assembled at Shiloh and pitched the Tabernacle of Testimony there. The land was held under their control.

    Read matching passage

  1. Numbers 6:3-5 Comparable consecration-vow restrictions with distinct wording

    Hannah's lifelong gift excludes wine, intoxicating drink and iron on the child's head; Numbers' consecration vow excludes wine and strong drink and forbids a razor during its stated term.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 6:3 from wine and strong drink, he shall be purified from wine. He shall drink neither vinegar of wine, nor vinegar of strong drink, nor anything prepared from grapes; and he shall eat neither fresh grapes nor raisins.

    Numbers 6:4 All the days of his vow he shall consume nothing made from the vine: wine, from pressed grape skins unto the seed.

    Numbers 6:5 All the days of the vow of purification no razor shall come upon his head. Until the days which he vowed unto the Lord are fulfilled, he shall be holy, letting the hair of his head grow long.

    Both accounts place the discovery of fallen Saul and his sons on Gelboue on the following day; 1 Samuel describes stripping the dead and Chronicles plundering the wounded, preserving the actual objects of the looting description.

They turned him over and stripped off his equipment. They sent it throughout the surrounding land of the foreigners, announcing the news to their idols and their people.

1 Samuel 31:11

The inhabitants of Jabesh in Gilead heard what the foreigners had done to Saul.

  1. 1 Samuel 11:9-11 Jabesh's hearing of Saul's fate recalls his earlier deliverance of that community

    The inhabitants of Jabesh in Gilead hear what happened to Saul; the earlier account names Jabesh's promised deliverance and Saul's attack on the Ammonite camp, supplying the particular prior community connection without declaring an unreported motive for the later retrieval.

1 Samuel 31:12

All the mighty men rose and traveled the whole night. They took Saul’s body and the body of Jonathan his son from the wall of Beth-shan, brought them to Jabesh, and burned them there.

  1. 1 Chronicles 10:12 Parallel retrieval reports with differing bodies and treatment details

    In 1 Samuel, the mighty men retrieve Saul and Jonathan from Beth-shan and burn them at Jabesh; Chronicles has mighty men from Galaad bring Saul and his sons to Jabis and bury their bones, keeping the different supplied extent, forms and burning detail.

  2. 2 Samuel 2:4-6 David's later blessing explicitly acknowledges Saul and Jonathan's burial

    The 1 Samuel account reports Jabesh's men retrieving Saul and Jonathan and taking them to Jabesh; David later blesses Jabesh's leaders for mercy shown by burying Saul and Jonathan, expressly recalling the named community's treatment of the two men.

    2 Samuel 2:4 Men of Judea came and anointed David there to reign over the house of Judah. They reported to David, “The men of Jabesh in Gilead buried Saul.”
